Some days I just pick at a piece of toast and have my coffee, and other days... well, I'll be at the grocery store—they rearranged the aisles at my local market, which is so confusing, I couldn't find the canned beans for twenty minutes—and I'll just stand in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. Well, I haven't really weighed myself... my scale in the bathroom is one of those old dial ones, you know? Guilty? No, I wouldn't say I feel guilty about it. It's just food, you know? Though they really do put so much sugar in things these days. I was actually watching this program on the television the other afternoon about how they process sugar cane down in South America. They have these massive machines—huge metal things, just churning away out in the fields. It reminded me of the factory my neighbor, Mr. Henderson, used to work at. He made parts for airplanes or something like that before he retired. He has this beautiful golden retriever named Buster who always barks at the mailman. The mailman is new, by the way, a very polite young man, he always makes sure to put the letters all the way in the box so they don't get wet. It rained so much last Tuesday, didn't it? My poor petunias were completely drowned out in the front yard. But anyway... no, I don't really feel bad after I eat the bakery things.

Well, like I was saying, it's just that my eating has been all turned around for months now. That's actually why I came in to see you. Because some days I just pick at my toast and have my coffee, and then other days... well, I'll find myself just standing right there in the bakery aisle at the grocery store, buying all these sweets that I don't even really enjoy eating. The grocery store, the one on Main Street, they just remodeled their whole bakery section. They put in these new glass display cases, very modern looking, with these bright lights that make everything look so shiny. It's almost too bright, really, it hurts my eyes a little bit if I look at it too long. My cousin Martha, she had terrible trouble with her eyes, she had to get those special tinted glasses... but yes, the eating is just completely turned around, so I suppose my weight might be doing the same thing, I just don't know for sure.

Well, decreased... I mean, I don't own a scale anymore. I threw it out years ago after I read this article in a magazine at the dentist's office. Dr. Evans is my dentist, he has the most beautiful saltwater aquarium in his waiting room. I could watch those little yellow fish swim around for hours. It's so calming, isn't it? Water just has that effect on people. My grandfather used to take me fishing down at the creek when I was a little girl... he had this old green tackle box that smelled like peppermint for some reason. 

But about my weight decreasing... it's just like I keep trying to explain, it's the eating that's the real problem. That's exactly why I made the appointment to come in here. My eating has just gotten all completely turned around for months now. I'll have days where I just sit there and pick at a piece of dry toast and sip my coffee, and I just can't bring myself to eat a real meal. And then, out of nowhere, other days I'll be standing right there in the middle of the bakery aisle, just filling my cart with all these sweets and pastries that I don't even really enjoy eating. It makes no sense at all. The bakery boxes are always tied with that little red string, though, which I always thought was a nice touch. I save the string sometimes to tie up the tomato plants in my garden... well, the ones the rabbits haven't gotten to yet, anyway.

You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, the activities... I still manage to get through the basic things for my aunt. I make sure she's settled. She has this lovely old armchair, it's upholstered in this floral pattern with tiny pink roses. It used to sit in my grandmother's sunroom. The sunroom had these big bay windows that let in all the morning light, and we'd sit there and drink tea... Earl Grey, mostly, though sometimes chamomile if it was later in the day. 

But, oh, because of the eating being so turned around... it has made things harder. Caregiving, and just staying involved in town, it all feels so much more difficult than before. I've missed a few of the church planning calls lately. We were supposed to be organizing the spring bake sale. Usually, I'm the one who coordinates the pie donations, because you have to make sure you don't end up with ten cherry pies and no apple. Apple is always the best seller, especially Mrs. Higgins' apple pie. She puts a secret ingredient in the crust—I think it's a splash of vinegar, but she won't tell a soul. 

I've also put off my projects for the county fair... and I've even had to ask the neighbors to cover things I used to handle so easily. Mr. Henderson next door, he's been so kind, he helped me with the trash bins last week. He has a golden retriever that just barks and barks at the mailman... the polite young man I mentioned earlier with the catalogs. But yes, it's just all gotten so much harder to keep up with.

I mean, I don't own a scale anymore. I threw it out years ago after I read this article in a magazine at the dentist's office. Dr. Evans is my dentist, he has the most beautiful saltwater aquarium in his waiting room. I could watch those little yellow fish swim around for hours. It's so calming, isn't it? Water just has that effect on people. My grandfather used to take me fishing down at the creek when I was a little girl... he had this old green tackle box that smelled like peppermint for some reason. \n\nBut about my weight decreasing... it's just like I keep trying to explain, it's the eating that's the real problem. That's exactly why I made the appointment to come in here. My eating has just gotten all completely turned around for months now. I'll have days where I just sit there and pick at a piece of dry toast and sip my coffee, and I just can't bring myself to eat a real meal. And then, out of nowhere, other days I'll be standing right there in the middle of the bakery aisle, just filling my cart with all these sweets and pastries that I don't even really enjoy eating. It makes no sense at all. The bakery boxes are always tied with that little red string, though, which I always thought was a nice touch. I save the string sometimes to tie up the tomato plants in my garden... well, the ones the rabbits haven't gotten to yet, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or find more difficult to do because of your eating habits?\n\nPatient: Well, the activities... I still manage to get through the basic things for my aunt. I make sure she's settled. She has this lovely old armchair, it's upholstered in this floral pattern with tiny pink roses. It used to sit in my grandmother's sunroom. The sunroom had these big bay windows that let in all the morning light, and we'd sit there and drink tea... Earl Grey, mostly, though sometimes chamomile if it was later in the day. \n\nBut, oh, because of the eating being so turned around... it has made things harder. Caregiving, and just staying involved in town, it all feels so much more difficult than before. I've missed a few of the church planning calls lately. We were supposed to be organizing the spring bake sale. Usually, I'm the one who coordinates the pie donations, because you have to make sure you don't end up with ten cherry pies and no apple. Apple is always the best seller, especially Mrs. Higgins' apple pie. She puts a secret ingredient in the crust—I think it's a splash of vinegar, but she won't tell a soul. \n\nI've also put off my projects for the county fair... and I've even had to ask the neighbors to cover things I used to handle so easily. Mr. Henderson next door, he's been so kind, he helped me with the trash bins last week. He has a golden retriever that just barks and barks at the mailman... the polite young man I mentioned earlier with the catalogs. But yes, it's just all gotten so much harder to keep up with.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre you ever afraid of gaining weight?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 50 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Afraid of gaining weight? No, I wouldn't say I'm afraid of it... I don't really think about it like that. I mean, my clothes still fit the same way they always have. I've had this one blue wool sweater for, oh, it must be fifteen years now. I bought it at a little boutique downtown that closed last spring. It's a shame, really, the woman who ran it, Eleanor, she always had the most beautiful silk scarves in the window. She moved to Florida to be closer to her daughter. Her daughter just had twins, a boy and a girl, can you imagine? Twins run in my family, actually. My great-aunt had twins... but no, I'm not afraid of putting on weight or anything like that. It's just the eating itself that's so upside down right now.

I'll have days where I just sit there and pick at a piece of dry toast and sip my coffee, and I just can't bring myself to eat a real meal. And then, out of nowhere, other days I'll be standing right there in the middle of the bakery aisle, just filling my cart with all these sweets and pastries that I don't even really enjoy eating. It makes no sense at all. The bakery boxes are always tied with that little red string, though, which I always thought was a nice touch. I save the string sometimes to tie up the tomato plants in my garden... well, the ones the rabbits haven't gotten to yet, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or find more difficult to do because of your eating habits?\n\nPatient: Well, the activities... I still manage to get through the basic things for my aunt. I make sure she's settled. She has this lovely old armchair, it's upholstered in this floral pattern with tiny pink roses. It used to sit in my grandmother's sunroom. The sunroom had these big bay windows that let in all the morning light, and we'd sit there and drink tea... Earl Grey, mostly, though sometimes chamomile if it was later in the day. \n\nBut, oh, because of the eating being so turned around... it has made things harder. Caregiving, and just staying involved in town, it all feels so much more difficult than before. I've missed a few of the church planning calls lately. We were supposed to be organizing the spring bake sale. Usually, I'm the one who coordinates the pie donations, because you have to make sure you don't end up with ten cherry pies and no apple. Apple is always the best seller, especially Mrs. Higgins' apple pie. She puts a secret ingredient in the crust—I think it's a splash of vinegar, but she won't tell a soul. \n\nI've also put off my projects for the county fair... and I've even had to ask the neighbors to cover things I used to handle so easily. Mr. Henderson next door, he's been so kind, he helped me with the trash bins last week. He has a golden retriever that just barks and barks at the mailman... the polite young man I mentioned earlier with the catalogs. But yes, it's just all gotten so much harder to keep up with.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? No, I wouldn't say I'm afraid of it... I don't really think about it like that. I mean, my clothes still fit the same way they always have. I've had this one blue wool sweater for, oh, it must be fifteen years now. I bought it at a little boutique downtown that closed last spring. It's a shame, really, the woman who ran it, Eleanor, she always had the most beautiful silk scarves in the window. She moved to Florida to be closer to her daughter. Her daughter just had twins, a boy and a girl, can you imagine? Twins run in my family, actually. My great-aunt had twins... but no, I'm not afraid of putting on weight or anything like that. It's just the eating itself that's so upside down right now.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your eating habits are influenced by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, no, it's not a fear of gaining weight at all. I mean, I just said I'm not afraid of that, didn't I? It's just... the eating itself is what's so strange. Like I said about the bakery aisle. And speaking of the grocery store, they completely rearranged the aisles last week. I went in for coffee—just the regular roast, nothing fancy, I don't understand all those flavored coffees they have now, hazelnut and vanilla and what have you. Coffee should just taste like coffee, don't you think? Anyway, I went in, and they had moved all the canned goods over to where the paper towels used to be. It took me twenty minutes just to find a can of crushed tomatoes for my aunt's soup. She likes the soup with the little star-shaped pasta, pastina, I think it's called. But no, the eating is just... all over the place. It has nothing to do with worrying about my weight.

She was over last week—she just got this new job at the bank downtown, the one with the big glass windows? I always tell her she needs to dress warmer for that office, they keep the AC blasting all year. But anyway, she noticed... I came in because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at a piece of toast and have my coffee, and other days... well, I'll be at the grocery store—they rearranged the aisles at my local market, which is so confusing, I couldn't find the canned beans for twenty minutes—and I'll just stand in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. It's just strange.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over these past months?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't really weighed myself... my scale in the bathroom is one of those old dial ones, you know? It belonged to my mother, and the little red line always seems to stick around the zero mark unless you tap it just right. She bought it at a yard sale back in the nineties from Mrs. Gable down the street. Mrs. Gable always had the best yard sales, she used to sell these beautiful embroidered tea towels... but anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, weight. I'm not sure if I've lost a lot of weight, it's just... like I said, the eating is all over the place. Some days it's just the toast, and then other days I'm buying all those bakery sweets. It's just turned around, you know? My clothes fit a little differently, maybe, but I mostly wear these loose cardigans now anyway because the draft in my house is terrible since they fixed the roof last spring...\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty after eating those sweets or other foods?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I wouldn't say I feel guilty about it. It's just food, you know? Though they really do put so much sugar in things these days. I was actually watching this program on the television the other afternoon about how they process sugar cane down in South America. They have these massive machines—huge metal things, just churning away out in the fields. It reminded me of the factory my neighbor, Mr. Henderson, used to work at. He made parts for airplanes or something like that before he retired. He has this beautiful golden retriever named Buster who always barks at the mailman. The mailman is new, by the way, a very polite young man, he always makes sure to put the letters all the way in the box so they don't get wet. It rained so much last Tuesday, didn't it? My poor petunias were completely drowned out in the front yard. But anyway... no, I don't really feel bad after I eat the bakery things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently or falling off in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, I mostly wear those loose cardigans anyway... I actually order them from this catalog that comes in the mail. The mail carrier—I mentioned him earlier, the polite young man—he always leaves the catalog right on the porch chair so it doesn't get ruined. They have the most beautiful knit sweaters in there for the autumn season. \n\nBut about my clothes falling off... no, they aren't falling off exactly. I did have to pull the belt a notch tighter on my brown slacks the other day, though. It just goes back to what I was telling you when I first came in, about my eating being so turned around these past few months. Because when you're just picking at toast and coffee some days, and then other days you're standing in the bakery aisle buying all those sweets you don't even really want... well, your body just doesn't know what to do. I wore those brown slacks to the pharmacy, actually. Brenda, the pharmacist, she just had a baby girl... they named her Lily, which I think is just so precious. My aunt had a dog named Lily once, a little white poodle...\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's just that my eating has been all turned around for months now. That's actually why I came in to see you. Because some days I just pick at my toast and have my coffee, and then other days... well, I'll find myself just standing right there in the bakery aisle at the grocery store, buying all these sweets that I don't even really enjoy eating. The grocery store, the one on Main Street, they just remodeled their whole bakery section. They put in these new glass display cases, very modern looking, with these bright lights that make everything look so shiny. It's almost too bright, really, it hurts my eyes a little bit if I look at it too long. My cousin Martha, she had terrible trouble with her eyes, she had to get those special tinted glasses... but yes, the eating is just completely turned around, so I suppose my weight might be doing the same thing, I just don't know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your weight has decreased since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, decreased... I mean, I don't own a scale anymore. I threw it out years ago after I read this article in a magazine at the dentist's office. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, decreased significantly? I wouldn't say my weight has dropped a whole lot, but I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at a piece of toast and have my coffee... speaking of coffee, I finally found my favorite mug. It had been missing for weeks. It's this sturdy ceramic one with a picture of a lighthouse on it. My sister bought it for me when we took a trip up the coast to Maine about ten years ago. Oh, the seafood there was just incredible. We had clam chowder at this little place right on the water, and you could hear the seagulls squawking outside. 

But yes, my eating is just a mess. Like I was saying, some days it's just toast, and other days... well, other days I find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. I bought a whole box of those frosted sugar cookies last week. The ones with the thick pink frosting and the sprinkles? They look so pretty in the package, but they always taste a bit like cardboard, don't they? I don't know why they still sell them. The bakery section used to be so much better when Mr. Miller ran it. He made these fresh cinnamon rolls every Saturday morning, and the whole store would smell like butter and spice. But he retired... oh, it must have been five years ago now. He bought an RV and decided to travel the country with his wife. I think they went to the Grand Canyon first. But anyway, my weight... I haven't really noticed a huge drop, it's just the eating itself that's so upside down.

I feel like we're going in circles a bit, doctor. Like I just said, no, I haven't noticed a big drop in my weight or anything like that. I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. It's just so unpredictable. Some days I just pick at my toast and coffee... which actually reminds me, I really need to get a new toaster. The lever on mine gets stuck halfway down, so you have to stand there and hold it down if you want the bread to actually brown. 

It's a heavy silver one, I think I got it at Sears years and years ago. Do you remember when Sears used to send out that massive catalog in the mail? The Wish Book, they called it. My mother used to let my sister and me circle things in it for Christmas with a red marker. I always circled the bicycles, even though we lived at the top of this terribly steep hill and riding a bike would have been completely impractical. We had a neighbor, Mr. Henderson, who tried to ride a ten-speed down that hill once and ended up right in Mrs. Gable's prize-winning rose bushes. She was furious. 

But anyway, no, I haven't lost a noticeable amount of weight. It's just the eating itself being so strange, going from barely managing toast to standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy.

It's just strange.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over these past months?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't really weighed myself... my scale in the bathroom is one of those old dial ones, you know? It belonged to my mother, and the little red line always seems to stick around the zero mark unless you tap it just right. She bought it at a yard sale back in the nineties from Mrs. Gable down the street. Mrs. Gable always had the best yard sales, she used to sell these beautiful embroidered tea towels... but anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, weight. I'm not sure if I've lost a lot of weight, it's just... like I said, the eating is all over the place. Some days it's just the toast, and then other days I'm buying all those bakery sweets. It's just turned around, you know? My clothes fit a little differently, maybe, but I mostly wear these loose cardigans now anyway because the draft in my house is terrible since they fixed the roof last spring...\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty after eating those sweets or other foods?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I wouldn't say I feel guilty about it. It's just food, you know? Though they really do put so much sugar in things these days. I was actually watching this program on the television the other afternoon about how they process sugar cane down in South America. They have these massive machines—huge metal things, just churning away out in the fields. It reminded me of the factory my neighbor, Mr. Henderson, used to work at. He made parts for airplanes or something like that before he retired. He has this beautiful golden retriever named Buster who always barks at the mailman. The mailman is new, by the way, a very polite young man, he always makes sure to put the letters all the way in the box so they don't get wet. It rained so much last Tuesday, didn't it? My poor petunias were completely drowned out in the front yard. But anyway... no, I don't really feel bad after I eat the bakery things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently or falling off in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, I mostly wear those loose cardigans anyway... I actually order them from this catalog that comes in the mail. The mail carrier—I mentioned him earlier, the polite young man—he always leaves the catalog right on the porch chair so it doesn't get ruined. They have the most beautiful knit sweaters in there for the autumn season. \n\nBut about my clothes falling off... no, they aren't falling off exactly. I did have to pull the belt a notch tighter on my brown slacks the other day, though. It just goes back to what I was telling you when I first came in, about my eating being so turned around these past few months. Because when you're just picking at toast and coffee some days, and then other days you're standing in the bakery aisle buying all those sweets you don't even really want... well, your body just doesn't know what to do. I wore those brown slacks to the pharmacy, actually. Brenda, the pharmacist, she just had a baby girl... they named her Lily, which I think is just so precious. My aunt had a dog named Lily once, a little white poodle...\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's just that my eating has been all turned around for months now. That's actually why I came in to see you. Because some days I just pick at my toast and have my coffee, and then other days... well, I'll find myself just standing right there in the bakery aisle at the grocery store, buying all these sweets that I don't even really enjoy eating. The grocery store, the one on Main Street, they just remodeled their whole bakery section. They put in these new glass display cases, very modern looking, with these bright lights that make everything look so shiny. It's almost too bright, really, it hurts my eyes a little bit if I look at it too long. My cousin Martha, she had terrible trouble with her eyes, she had to get those special tinted glasses... but yes, the eating is just completely turned around, so I suppose my weight might be doing the same thing, I just don't know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your weight has decreased since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, decreased... I mean, I don't own a scale anymore. I threw it out years ago after I read this article in a magazine at the dentist's office. Dr. Evans is my dentist, he has the most beautiful saltwater aquarium in his waiting room. I could watch those little yellow fish swim around for hours. It's so calming, isn't it? Water just has that effect on people. My grandfather used to take me fishing down at the creek when I was a little girl... he had this old green tackle box that smelled like peppermint for some reason. \n\nBut about my weight decreasing... it's just like I keep trying to explain, it's the eating that's the real problem. That's exactly why I made the appointment to come in here. My eating has just gotten all completely turned around for months now. I'll have days where I just sit there and pick at a piece of dry toast and sip my coffee, and I just can't bring myself to eat a real meal. And then, out of nowhere, other days I'll be standing right there in the middle of the bakery aisle, just filling my cart with all these sweets and pastries that I don't even really enjoy eating. It makes no sense at all. The bakery boxes are always tied with that little red string, though, which I always thought was a nice touch. I save the string sometimes to tie up the tomato plants in my garden... well, the ones the rabbits haven't gotten to yet, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or find more difficult to do because of your eating habits?\n\nPatient: Well, the activities... I still manage to get through the basic things for my aunt. I make sure she's settled. She has this lovely old armchair, it's upholstered in this floral pattern with tiny pink roses. It used to sit in my grandmother's sunroom. The sunroom had these big bay windows that let in all the morning light, and we'd sit there and drink tea... Earl Grey, mostly, though sometimes chamomile if it was later in the day. \n\nBut, oh, because of the eating being so turned around... it has made things harder. Caregiving, and just staying involved in town, it all feels so much more difficult than before. I've missed a few of the church planning calls lately. We were supposed to be organizing the spring bake sale. Usually, I'm the one who coordinates the pie donations, because you have to make sure you don't end up with ten cherry pies and no apple. Apple is always the best seller, especially Mrs. Higgins' apple pie. She puts a secret ingredient in the crust—I think it's a splash of vinegar, but she won't tell a soul. \n\nI've also put off my projects for the county fair... and I've even had to ask the neighbors to cover things I used to handle so easily. Mr. Henderson next door, he's been so kind, he helped me with the trash bins last week. He has a golden retriever that just barks and barks at the mailman... the polite young man I mentioned earlier with the catalogs. But yes, it's just all gotten so much harder to keep up with.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? No, I wouldn't say I'm afraid of it... I don't really think about it like that. I mean, my clothes still fit the same way they always have. I've had this one blue wool sweater for, oh, it must be fifteen years now. I bought it at a little boutique downtown that closed last spring. It's a shame, really, the woman who ran it, Eleanor, she always had the most beautiful silk scarves in the window. She moved to Florida to be closer to her daughter. Her daughter just had twins, a boy and a girl, can you imagine? Twins run in my family, actually. My great-aunt had twins... but no, I'm not afraid of putting on weight or anything like that. It's just the eating itself that's so upside down right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are influenced by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's not a fear of gaining weight at all. I mean, I just said I'm not afraid of that, didn't I? It's just... the eating itself is what's so strange. Like I said about the bakery aisle. And speaking of the grocery store, they completely rearranged the aisles last week. I went in for coffee—just the regular roast, nothing fancy, I don't understand all those flavored coffees they have now, hazelnut and vanilla and what have you. Coffee should just taste like coffee, don't you think? Anyway, I went in, and they had moved all the canned goods over to where the paper towels used to be. It took me twenty minutes just to find a can of crushed tomatoes for my aunt's soup. She likes the soup with the little star-shaped pasta, pastina, I think it's called. But no, the eating is just... all over the place. It has nothing to do with worrying about my weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your weight has decreased significantly since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, decreased significantly? I wouldn't say my weight has dropped a whole lot, but I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at a piece of toast and have my coffee... speaking of coffee, I finally found my favorite mug. It had been missing for weeks. It's this sturdy ceramic one with a picture of a lighthouse on it. My sister bought it for me when we took a trip up the coast to Maine about ten years ago. Oh, the seafood there was just incredible. We had clam chowder at this little place right on the water, and you could hear the seagulls squawking outside. \n\nBut yes, my eating is just a mess. Like I was saying, some days it's just toast, and other days... well, other days I find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. I bought a whole box of those frosted sugar cookies last week. The ones with the thick pink frosting and the sprinkles? They look so pretty in the package, but they always taste a bit like cardboard, don't they? I don't know why they still sell them. The bakery section used to be so much better when Mr. Miller ran it. He made these fresh cinnamon rolls every Saturday morning, and the whole store would smell like butter and spice. But he retired... oh, it must have been five years ago now. He bought an RV and decided to travel the country with his wife. I think they went to the Grand Canyon first. But anyway, my weight... I haven't really noticed a huge drop, it's just the eating itself that's so upside down.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I feel like we're going in circles a bit, doctor. Like I just said, no, I haven't noticed a big drop in my weight or anything like that. I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. It's just so unpredictable. Some days I just pick at my toast and coffee... which actually reminds me, I really need to get a new toaster. The lever on mine gets stuck halfway down, so you have to stand there and hold it down if you want the bread to actually brown. \n\nIt's a heavy silver one, I think I got it at Sears years and years ago. Do you remember when Sears used to send out that massive catalog in the mail? The Wish Book, they called it. My mother used to let my sister and me circle things in it for Christmas with a red marker. I always circled the bicycles, even though we lived at the top of this terribly steep hill and riding a bike would have been completely impractical. We had a neighbor, Mr. Henderson, who tried to ride a ten-speed down that hill once and ended up right in Mrs. Gable's prize-winning rose bushes. She was furious. \n\nBut anyway, no, I haven't lost a noticeable amount of weight. It's just the eating itself being so strange, going from barely managing toast to standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

{"candidates": ["F50.8", "F50.9"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

It has nothing to do with worrying about my weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your weight has decreased significantly since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, decreased significantly? I wouldn't say my weight has dropped a whole lot, but I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at a piece of toast and have my coffee... speaking of coffee, I finally found my favorite mug. It had been missing for weeks. It's this sturdy ceramic one with a picture of a lighthouse on it. My sister bought it for me when we took a trip up the coast to Maine about ten years ago. Oh, the seafood there was just incredible. We had clam chowder at this little place right on the water, and you could hear the seagulls squawking outside. \n\nBut yes, my eating is just a mess. Like I was saying, some days it's just toast, and other days... well, other days I find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. I bought a whole box of those frosted sugar cookies last week. The ones with the thick pink frosting and the sprinkles? They look so pretty in the package, but they always taste a bit like cardboard, don't they? I don't know why they still sell them. The bakery section used to be so much better when Mr. Miller ran it. He made these fresh cinnamon rolls every Saturday morning, and the whole store would smell like butter and spice. But he retired... oh, it must have been five years ago now. He bought an RV and decided to travel the country with his wife. I think they went to the Grand Canyon first. But anyway, my weight... I haven't really noticed a huge drop, it's just the eating itself that's so upside down.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I feel like we're going in circles a bit, doctor. Like I just said, no, I haven't noticed a big drop in my weight or anything like that. I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. It's just so unpredictable. Some days I just pick at my toast and coffee... which actually reminds me, I really need to get a new toaster. The lever on mine gets stuck halfway down, so you have to stand there and hold it down if you want the bread to actually brown. \n\nIt's a heavy silver one, I think I got it at Sears years and years ago. Do you remember when Sears used to send out that massive catalog in the mail? The Wish Book, they called it. My mother used to let my sister and me circle things in it for Christmas with a red marker. I always circled the bicycles, even though we lived at the top of this terribly steep hill and riding a bike would have been completely impractical. We had a neighbor, Mr. Henderson, who tried to ride a ten-speed down that hill once and ended up right in Mrs. Gable's prize-winning rose bushes. She was furious. \n\nBut anyway, no, I haven't lost a noticeable amount of weight. It's just the eating itself being so strange, going from barely managing toast to standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you find that your eating habits change in response to your emotions?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Emotions? Oh, I don't know if it's emotions. I just... I came in because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, like I've been trying to explain. It's just so erratic. Like I said, some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I'm standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. 

Speaking of sweets, my neighbor brought over a fruitcake for the holidays last year. I know people always make jokes about fruitcake, how it's heavy enough to use as a doorstop and all that, but this one actually wasn't bad. She soaked it in rum, I think. Or maybe brandy. Her husband, Tom, he collects vintage decanters, so they always have some kind of liquor sitting out on that beautiful mahogany sideboard they have in their dining room. I've always wanted a sideboard like that, but my dining room is much too small. If I put one in there, you wouldn't even be able to pull the chairs out to sit down! We'd all have to eat standing up, like we were at a cocktail party or something. 

But anyway, no, I don't think it's about my emotions. It's just that my appetite is completely upside down these days.

But anyway... no, I don't really feel bad after I eat the bakery things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently or falling off in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, I mostly wear those loose cardigans anyway... I actually order them from this catalog that comes in the mail. I mean, I don't own a scale anymore. I threw it out years ago after I read this article in a magazine at the dentist's office. Dr. Evans is my dentist, he has the most beautiful saltwater aquarium in his waiting room. I could watch those little yellow fish swim around for hours. It's so calming, isn't it? Water just has that effect on people. My grandfather used to take me fishing down at the creek when I was a little girl... he had this old green tackle box that smelled like peppermint for some reason. \n\nBut about my weight decreasing... it's just like I keep trying to explain, it's the eating that's the real problem. That's exactly why I made the appointment to come in here. My eating has just gotten all completely turned around for months now. I'll have days where I just sit there and pick at a piece of dry toast and sip my coffee, and I just can't bring myself to eat a real meal. And then, out of nowhere, other days I'll be standing right there in the middle of the bakery aisle, just filling my cart with all these sweets and pastries that I don't even really enjoy eating. It makes no sense at all. The bakery boxes are always tied with that little red string, though, which I always thought was a nice touch. I save the string sometimes to tie up the tomato plants in my garden... well, the ones the rabbits haven't gotten to yet, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or find more difficult to do because of your eating habits?\n\nPatient: Well, the activities... I still manage to get through the basic things for my aunt. I make sure she's settled. She has this lovely old armchair, it's upholstered in this floral pattern with tiny pink roses. Earl Grey, mostly, though sometimes chamomile if it was later in the day. \n\nBut, oh, because of the eating being so turned around... it has made things harder. We were supposed to be organizing the spring bake sale. Usually, I'm the one who coordinates the pie donations, because you have to make sure you don't end up with ten cherry pies and no apple. Apple is always the best seller, especially Mrs. Higgins' apple pie. She puts a secret ingredient in the crust—I think it's a splash of vinegar, but she won't tell a soul. \n\nI've also put off my projects for the county fair... and I've even had to ask the neighbors to cover things I used to handle so easily. Mr. Henderson next door, he's been so kind, he helped me with the trash bins last week. He has a golden retriever that just barks and barks at the mailman... the polite young man I mentioned earlier with the catalogs. But yes, it's just all gotten so much harder to keep up with.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? No, I wouldn't say I'm afraid of it... I bought it at a little boutique downtown that closed last spring. It's a shame, really, the woman who ran it, Eleanor, she always had the most beautiful silk scarves in the window. She moved to Florida to be closer to her daughter. Her daughter just had twins, a boy and a girl, can you imagine? Twins run in my family, actually. My great-aunt had twins... but no, I'm not afraid of putting on weight or anything like that. It's just the eating itself that's so upside down right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are influenced by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's not a fear of gaining weight at all. They have the most beautiful knit sweaters in there for the autumn season. \n\nBut about my clothes falling off... no, they aren't falling off exactly. I did have to pull the belt a notch tighter on my brown slacks the other day, though. It just goes back to what I was telling you when I first came in, about my eating being so turned around these past few months. Because when you're just picking at toast and coffee some days, and then other days you're standing in the bakery aisle buying all those sweets you don't even really want... well, your body just doesn't know what to do. I wore those brown slacks to the pharmacy, actually. Brenda, the pharmacist, she just had a baby girl... they named her Lily, which I think is just so precious. My aunt had a dog named Lily once, a little white poodle...\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's just that my eating has been all turned around for months now. That's actually why I came in to see you. Because some days I just pick at my toast and have my coffee, and then other days... well, I'll find myself just standing right there in the bakery aisle at the grocery store, buying all these sweets that I don't even really enjoy eating. The grocery store, the one on Main Street, they just remodeled their whole bakery section. They put in these new glass display cases, very modern looking, with these bright lights that make everything look so shiny. It's almost too bright, really, it hurts my eyes a little bit if I look at it too long. My cousin Martha, she had terrible trouble with her eyes, she had to get those special tinted glasses... but yes, the eating is just completely turned around, so I suppose my weight might be doing the same thing, I just don't know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your weight has decreased since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, decreased... I mean, I don't own a scale anymore. I threw it out years ago after I read this article in a magazine at the dentist's office. Dr. Evans is my dentist, he has the most beautiful saltwater aquarium in his waiting room. I could watch those little yellow fish swim around for hours. It's so calming, isn't it? Water just has that effect on people. My grandfather used to take me fishing down at the creek when I was a little girl... he had this old green tackle box that smelled like peppermint for some reason. \n\nBut about my weight decreasing... it's just like I keep trying to explain, it's the eating that's the real problem. That's exactly why I made the appointment to come in here. My eating has just gotten all completely turned around for months now. I'll have days where I just sit there and pick at a piece of dry toast and sip my coffee, and I just can't bring myself to eat a real meal. And then, out of nowhere, other days I'll be standing right there in the middle of the bakery aisle, just filling my cart with all these sweets and pastries that I don't even really enjoy eating. It makes no sense at all. The bakery boxes are always tied with that little red string, though, which I always thought was a nice touch. I save the string sometimes to tie up the tomato plants in my garden... well, the ones the rabbits haven't gotten to yet, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or find more difficult to do because of your eating habits?\n\nPatient: Well, the activities... I still manage to get through the basic things for my aunt. I make sure she's settled. She has this lovely old armchair, it's upholstered in this floral pattern with tiny pink roses. It used to sit in my grandmother's sunroom. The sunroom had these big bay windows that let in all the morning light, and we'd sit there and drink tea... Earl Grey, mostly, though sometimes chamomile if it was later in the day. \n\nBut, oh, because of the eating being so turned around... it has made things harder. Caregiving, and just staying involved in town, it all feels so much more difficult than before. I've missed a few of the church planning calls lately. We were supposed to be organizing the spring bake sale. Usually, I'm the one who coordinates the pie donations, because you have to make sure you don't end up with ten cherry pies and no apple. Apple is always the best seller, especially Mrs. Higgins' apple pie. She puts a secret ingredient in the crust—I think it's a splash of vinegar, but she won't tell a soul. \n\nI've also put off my projects for the county fair... and I've even had to ask the neighbors to cover things I used to handle so easily. Mr. Henderson next door, he's been so kind, he helped me with the trash bins last week. He has a golden retriever that just barks and barks at the mailman... the polite young man I mentioned earlier with the catalogs. But yes, it's just all gotten so much harder to keep up with.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? No, I wouldn't say I'm afraid of it... I don't really think about it like that. I mean, my clothes still fit the same way they always have. I've had this one blue wool sweater for, oh, it must be fifteen years now. I bought it at a little boutique downtown that closed last spring. It's a shame, really, the woman who ran it, Eleanor, she always had the most beautiful silk scarves in the window. She moved to Florida to be closer to her daughter. Her daughter just had twins, a boy and a girl, can you imagine? Twins run in my family, actually. My great-aunt had twins... but no, I'm not afraid of putting on weight or anything like that. It's just the eating itself that's so upside down right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are influenced by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's not a fear of gaining weight at all. I mean, I just said I'm not afraid of that, didn't I? It's just... the eating itself is what's so strange. Like I said about the bakery aisle. And speaking of the grocery store, they completely rearranged the aisles last week. I went in for coffee—just the regular roast, nothing fancy, I don't understand all those flavored coffees they have now, hazelnut and vanilla and what have you. Coffee should just taste like coffee, don't you think? Anyway, I went in, and they had moved all the canned goods over to where the paper towels used to be. It took me twenty minutes just to find a can of crushed tomatoes for my aunt's soup. She likes the soup with the little star-shaped pasta, pastina, I think it's called. But no, the eating is just... all over the place. It has nothing to do with worrying about my weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your weight has decreased significantly since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, decreased significantly? I wouldn't say my weight has dropped a whole lot, but I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at a piece of toast and have my coffee... speaking of coffee, I finally found my favorite mug. It had been missing for weeks. It's this sturdy ceramic one with a picture of a lighthouse on it. My sister bought it for me when we took a trip up the coast to Maine about ten years ago. Oh, the seafood there was just incredible. We had clam chowder at this little place right on the water, and you could hear the seagulls squawking outside. \n\nBut yes, my eating is just a mess. Like I was saying, some days it's just toast, and other days... well, other days I find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. I bought a whole box of those frosted sugar cookies last week. The ones with the thick pink frosting and the sprinkles? They look so pretty in the package, but they always taste a bit like cardboard, don't they? I don't know why they still sell them. The bakery section used to be so much better when Mr. Miller ran it. He made these fresh cinnamon rolls every Saturday morning, and the whole store would smell like butter and spice. But he retired... oh, it must have been five years ago now. He bought an RV and decided to travel the country with his wife. I think they went to the Grand Canyon first. But anyway, my weight... I haven't really noticed a huge drop, it's just the eating itself that's so upside down.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I feel like we're going in circles a bit, doctor. Like I just said, no, I haven't noticed a big drop in my weight or anything like that. I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. It's just so unpredictable. Some days I just pick at my toast and coffee... which actually reminds me, I really need to get a new toaster. The lever on mine gets stuck halfway down, so you have to stand there and hold it down if you want the bread to actually brown. \n\nIt's a heavy silver one, I think I got it at Sears years and years ago. Do you remember when Sears used to send out that massive catalog in the mail? The Wish Book, they called it. My mother used to let my sister and me circle things in it for Christmas with a red marker. I always circled the bicycles, even though we lived at the top of this terribly steep hill and riding a bike would have been completely impractical. We had a neighbor, Mr. Henderson, who tried to ride a ten-speed down that hill once and ended up right in Mrs. Gable's prize-winning rose bushes. She was furious. \n\nBut anyway, no, I haven't lost a noticeable amount of weight. It's just the eating itself being so strange, going from barely managing toast to standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating habits change in response to your emotions?\n\nPatient: Emotions? Oh, I don't know if it's emotions. I just... I came in because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, like I've been trying to explain. It's just so erratic. Like I said, some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I'm standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. \n\nSpeaking of sweets, my neighbor brought over a fruitcake for the holidays last year. I know people always make jokes about fruitcake, how it's heavy enough to use as a doorstop and all that, but this one actually wasn't bad. She soaked it in rum, I think. Or maybe brandy. Her husband, Tom, he collects vintage decanters, so they always have some kind of liquor sitting out on that beautiful mahogany sideboard they have in their dining room. I've always wanted a sideboard like that, but my dining room is much too small. If I put one in there, you wouldn't even be able to pull the chairs out to sit down! We'd all have to eat standing up, like we were at a cocktail party or something. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't think it's about my emotions. It's just that my appetite is completely upside down these days.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Doctor, I feel like I keep saying the same thing over and over. I don't know about my mood affecting it. I just came in because my eating has been completely turned around for months now. Like I've mentioned, some days I just pick at my toast and coffee, and other days I'm in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even like. 

Speaking of the bakery, I saw the strangest thing there the other day. A woman had her little dog in the shopping cart, right where you put the groceries! It was one of those tiny, fluffy white dogs—a Bichon Frise, maybe? My aunt Edna used to have a Bichon. She named him Snowflake, which was terribly unoriginal, but she loved that dog more than anything. She used to knit him little sweaters for the winter. Bright red ones, usually. Edna was always knitting. She made me a scarf once that was so long it wrapped around my neck three times and still dragged on the floor. I wore it anyway, just to make her happy when she visited. 

But no, I don't think it's my mood. It's just my eating being so upside down, going from toast to sweets for no reason at all.

She bought it at a yard sale back in the nineties from Mrs. Gable down the street. Mrs. Gable always had the best yard sales, she used to sell these beautiful embroidered tea towels... but anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, weight. I'm not sure if I've lost a lot of weight, it's just... like I said, the eating is all over the place. Some days it's just the toast, and then other days I'm buying all those bakery sweets. It's just turned around, you know? My clothes fit a little differently, maybe, but I mostly wear these loose cardigans now anyway because the draft in my house is terrible since they fixed the roof last spring...\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty after eating those sweets or other foods?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I wouldn't say I feel guilty about it. It's just food, you know? Though they really do put so much sugar in things these days. I was actually watching this program on the television the other afternoon about how they process sugar cane down in South America. They have these massive machines—huge metal things, just churning away out in the fields. It reminded me of the factory my neighbor, Mr. Henderson, used to work at. He made parts for airplanes or something like that before he retired. He has this beautiful golden retriever named Buster who always barks at the mailman. The mailman is new, by the way, a very polite young man, he always makes sure to put the letters all the way in the box so they don't get wet. It rained so much last Tuesday, didn't it? My poor petunias were completely drowned out in the front yard. But anyway... no, I don't really feel bad after I eat the bakery things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently or falling off in the past few months?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, I mostly wear those loose cardigans anyway... I actually order them from this catalog that comes in the mail. The mail carrier—I mentioned him earlier, the polite young man—he always leaves the catalog right on the porch chair so it doesn't get ruined. They have the most beautiful knit sweaters in there for the autumn season. \n\nBut about my clothes falling off... no, they aren't falling off exactly. I did have to pull the belt a notch tighter on my brown slacks the other day, though. It just goes back to what I was telling you when I first came in, about my eating being so turned around these past few months. Because when you're just picking at toast and coffee some days, and then other days you're standing in the bakery aisle buying all those sweets you don't even really want... well, your body just doesn't know what to do. I wore those brown slacks to the pharmacy, actually. Brenda, the pharmacist, she just had a baby girl... they named her Lily, which I think is just so precious. My aunt had a dog named Lily once, a little white poodle...\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's just that my eating has been all turned around for months now. That's actually why I came in to see you. Because some days I just pick at my toast and have my coffee, and then other days... well, I'll find myself just standing right there in the bakery aisle at the grocery store, buying all these sweets that I don't even really enjoy eating. The grocery store, the one on Main Street, they just remodeled their whole bakery section. They put in these new glass display cases, very modern looking, with these bright lights that make everything look so shiny. It's almost too bright, really, it hurts my eyes a little bit if I look at it too long. My cousin Martha, she had terrible trouble with her eyes, she had to get those special tinted glasses... but yes, the eating is just completely turned around, so I suppose my weight might be doing the same thing, I just don't know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your weight has decreased since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, decreased... I mean, I don't own a scale anymore. I threw it out years ago after I read this article in a magazine at the dentist's office. Dr. Evans is my dentist, he has the most beautiful saltwater aquarium in his waiting room. I could watch those little yellow fish swim around for hours. It's so calming, isn't it? Water just has that effect on people. My grandfather used to take me fishing down at the creek when I was a little girl... he had this old green tackle box that smelled like peppermint for some reason. \n\nBut about my weight decreasing... it's just like I keep trying to explain, it's the eating that's the real problem. That's exactly why I made the appointment to come in here. My eating has just gotten all completely turned around for months now. I'll have days where I just sit there and pick at a piece of dry toast and sip my coffee, and I just can't bring myself to eat a real meal. And then, out of nowhere, other days I'll be standing right there in the middle of the bakery aisle, just filling my cart with all these sweets and pastries that I don't even really enjoy eating. It makes no sense at all. The bakery boxes are always tied with that little red string, though, which I always thought was a nice touch. I save the string sometimes to tie up the tomato plants in my garden... well, the ones the rabbits haven't gotten to yet, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities you've stopped doing or find more difficult to do because of your eating habits?\n\nPatient: Well, the activities... I still manage to get through the basic things for my aunt. I make sure she's settled. She has this lovely old armchair, it's upholstered in this floral pattern with tiny pink roses. It used to sit in my grandmother's sunroom. The sunroom had these big bay windows that let in all the morning light, and we'd sit there and drink tea... Earl Grey, mostly, though sometimes chamomile if it was later in the day. \n\nBut, oh, because of the eating being so turned around... it has made things harder. Caregiving, and just staying involved in town, it all feels so much more difficult than before. I've missed a few of the church planning calls lately. We were supposed to be organizing the spring bake sale. Usually, I'm the one who coordinates the pie donations, because you have to make sure you don't end up with ten cherry pies and no apple. Apple is always the best seller, especially Mrs. Higgins' apple pie. She puts a secret ingredient in the crust—I think it's a splash of vinegar, but she won't tell a soul. \n\nI've also put off my projects for the county fair... and I've even had to ask the neighbors to cover things I used to handle so easily. Mr. Henderson next door, he's been so kind, he helped me with the trash bins last week. He has a golden retriever that just barks and barks at the mailman... the polite young man I mentioned earlier with the catalogs. But yes, it's just all gotten so much harder to keep up with.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Afraid of gaining weight? No, I wouldn't say I'm afraid of it... I don't really think about it like that. I mean, my clothes still fit the same way they always have. I've had this one blue wool sweater for, oh, it must be fifteen years now. I bought it at a little boutique downtown that closed last spring. It's a shame, really, the woman who ran it, Eleanor, she always had the most beautiful silk scarves in the window. She moved to Florida to be closer to her daughter. Her daughter just had twins, a boy and a girl, can you imagine? Twins run in my family, actually. My great-aunt had twins... but no, I'm not afraid of putting on weight or anything like that. It's just the eating itself that's so upside down right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are influenced by a fear of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's not a fear of gaining weight at all. I mean, I just said I'm not afraid of that, didn't I? It's just... the eating itself is what's so strange. Like I said about the bakery aisle. And speaking of the grocery store, they completely rearranged the aisles last week. I went in for coffee—just the regular roast, nothing fancy, I don't understand all those flavored coffees they have now, hazelnut and vanilla and what have you. Coffee should just taste like coffee, don't you think? Anyway, I went in, and they had moved all the canned goods over to where the paper towels used to be. It took me twenty minutes just to find a can of crushed tomatoes for my aunt's soup. She likes the soup with the little star-shaped pasta, pastina, I think it's called. But no, the eating is just... all over the place. It has nothing to do with worrying about my weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your weight has decreased significantly since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: Well, decreased significantly? I wouldn't say my weight has dropped a whole lot, but I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. Some days I just pick at a piece of toast and have my coffee... speaking of coffee, I finally found my favorite mug. It had been missing for weeks. It's this sturdy ceramic one with a picture of a lighthouse on it. My sister bought it for me when we took a trip up the coast to Maine about ten years ago. Oh, the seafood there was just incredible. We had clam chowder at this little place right on the water, and you could hear the seagulls squawking outside. \n\nBut yes, my eating is just a mess. Like I was saying, some days it's just toast, and other days... well, other days I find myself standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. I bought a whole box of those frosted sugar cookies last week. The ones with the thick pink frosting and the sprinkles? They look so pretty in the package, but they always taste a bit like cardboard, don't they? I don't know why they still sell them. The bakery section used to be so much better when Mr. Miller ran it. He made these fresh cinnamon rolls every Saturday morning, and the whole store would smell like butter and spice. But he retired... oh, it must have been five years ago now. He bought an RV and decided to travel the country with his wife. I think they went to the Grand Canyon first. But anyway, my weight... I haven't really noticed a huge drop, it's just the eating itself that's so upside down.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I feel like we're going in circles a bit, doctor. Like I just said, no, I haven't noticed a big drop in my weight or anything like that. I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. It's just so unpredictable. Some days I just pick at my toast and coffee... which actually reminds me, I really need to get a new toaster. The lever on mine gets stuck halfway down, so you have to stand there and hold it down if you want the bread to actually brown. \n\nIt's a heavy silver one, I think I got it at Sears years and years ago. Do you remember when Sears used to send out that massive catalog in the mail? The Wish Book, they called it. My mother used to let my sister and me circle things in it for Christmas with a red marker. I always circled the bicycles, even though we lived at the top of this terribly steep hill and riding a bike would have been completely impractical. We had a neighbor, Mr. Henderson, who tried to ride a ten-speed down that hill once and ended up right in Mrs. Gable's prize-winning rose bushes. She was furious. \n\nBut anyway, no, I haven't lost a noticeable amount of weight. It's just the eating itself being so strange, going from barely managing toast to standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating habits change in response to your emotions?\n\nPatient: Emotions? Oh, I don't know if it's emotions. I just... I came in because my eating has gotten all turned around for months now, like I've been trying to explain. It's just so erratic. Like I said, some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I'm standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. \n\nSpeaking of sweets, my neighbor brought over a fruitcake for the holidays last year. I know people always make jokes about fruitcake, how it's heavy enough to use as a doorstop and all that, but this one actually wasn't bad. She soaked it in rum, I think. Or maybe brandy. Her husband, Tom, he collects vintage decanters, so they always have some kind of liquor sitting out on that beautiful mahogany sideboard they have in their dining room. I've always wanted a sideboard like that, but my dining room is much too small. If I put one in there, you wouldn't even be able to pull the chairs out to sit down! We'd all have to eat standing up, like we were at a cocktail party or something. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't think it's about my emotions. It's just that my appetite is completely upside down these days.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mood affects how much or what you eat?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like I keep saying the same thing over and over. I don't know about my mood affecting it. I just came in because my eating has been completely turned around for months now. Like I've mentioned, some days I just pick at my toast and coffee, and other days I'm in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even like. \n\nSpeaking of the bakery, I saw the strangest thing there the other day. A woman had her little dog in the shopping cart, right where you put the groceries! It was one of those tiny, fluffy white dogs—a Bichon Frise, maybe? My aunt Edna used to have a Bichon. She named him Snowflake, which was terribly unoriginal, but she loved that dog more than anything. She used to knit him little sweaters for the winter. Bright red ones, usually. Edna was always knitting. She made me a scarf once that was so long it wrapped around my neck three times and still dragged on the floor. I wore it anyway, just to make her happy when she visited. \n\nBut no, I don't think it's my mood. It's just my eating being so upside down, going from toast to sweets for no reason at all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doctor, I feel like you're not hearing me. It's not about trying to avoid gaining weight or not being interested. Like I've been saying, I came in because my eating has just gotten all turned around for months now. It's completely unpredictable. Some days I just pick at toast and coffee, and other days I'm standing in the bakery aisle buying sweets I don't even really enjoy. 

I bought these lemon squares last time. I don't even like lemon! It always reminds me of the furniture polish my grandmother used to use. She had this beautiful antique dining set, cherry wood, I think. She made us polish it every single Sunday before dinner. We'd have to rub it in little circles until our arms ached. If you missed a spot, she'd know. She had eyes like a hawk, that woman. She could spot a speck of dust from across the room. She used to say, "A clean house is a sign of a clear mind." I always wondered if that meant my mind was a mess, because my house is definitely not spotless right now. I keep meaning to dust the living room, but I just look at the Swiffer and think... what's the point? It's just going to get dusty again anyway. 

But anyway, no, I'm not trying to avoid gaining weight. My eating is just completely upside down.
